## Throw-augmenting items  ← opened 2026-07-22 (user concepts delivered; engine recon done; design discussion in progress)

### Thrown-system ground truth (decompiled 2026-07-22; full-assembly dump in session scratchpad)
- **There is no to-hit roll vs DV for ordinary thrown weapons.** `GameObject.PerformThrow`: a throw rolls a *hit budget* = `RollPenetratingSuccesses("1d" + Agility, 3)` (budget spent as `-= distance` per connect), and an **AimVariance** scatter roll = `Random(0,20) − 10`, pulled toward 0 by the Agility modifier. The projectile walks the trajectory; budget > 0 + a combat target in a cell = hit. Accuracy in Qud throwing = *scatter*, not a hit roll.
- **`ThrowToHitBonus`/`ThrowToHitSkillBonus` are consumed by exactly one thing in the game: GeomagneticDisc** (rolls its own Agility+properties attack). For ordinary rocks these properties are dead knobs — Deft Throwing's "+2 to hit" and the cybernetic's ThrowToHitBonus:2 only fire when throwing the disc. **Do not hang item design on ThrowToHitBonus.**
- **The real accuracy knob: `CloseThrowRangeAccuracyBonus` / `...SkillBonus`** (actor int properties, engine takes the max): when target distance ≤ throw Range, `AimVariance × (100 − bonus)/100` — at 100, zero scatter ("your throwing accuracy is 100%", the base cybernetic's own wording). Outside Range it never applies and throws also gain fall-short/long distance variance — so **range bonuses ARE accuracy bonuses** (they widen the perfect-accuracy zone).
- **Range**: base 4 + `ThrowRangeBonus` (read from BOTH actor property and thrown-item property) + `ThrowRangeSkillBonus` + Strength score modifier (`GetThrowProfileEvent.Process`).
- **Damage**: penetration modifier = `max(Strength mod, 1)`; Vorpal → pen = AV; then dice × penetrations (`RollDamagePenetrations`, the eb_sim.py core). **The damage/pen hook (`GetThrownWeaponPerformanceEvent`: DamageDieModifier/DamageModifier/PenetrationBonus fields) dispatches to the thrown OBJECT only — worn gear has no native thrown-damage hook.** Actor-side damage paths: Strength buff (boosts pen natively, crude), or mod-side EB integration (the mutation sculpts its own stones and can read actor state).
- **Benchmarks**: Deft Throwing skill = +3 range, +50 close-accuracy (50 Agi cost); **parabolic muscular subroutine** (2-point Arm cybernetic) = +2 range, 100 close-accuracy — the stabilizer concept's direct base-game cousin, mind the overlap; force knife = item-side ThrowRangeBonus +6.
- **Equipment vehicles, all verified**: `IntPropertyChanger` (generic worn-item property applicator, `WorksOnWearer` default true, IPoweredPart so `ChargeUse="0"` = unpowered — the XML-only route for stabilizers/visor passives); AjiConch = the clean template for an item-activated ability (AddDynamicCommand on equip, cooldown, AI advertisement); timed buffs = standard Effect with Duration (Luminous pattern). Slots: Arm (BaseBracelet/BaseArmlet/LeatherBracer), Face (BaseEyewear; **"Telemetric Visor" already exists** — techscan HUD, name-adjacent). Consumable/refill patterns available: stacked consumables, liquid reservoir (torch pattern), energy cells (tech flavor).
- **Sling architecture fork (user to rule):** (a) worn throw-booster (property route: range trivially, damage only via the caveats above) vs (b) a true `MissileWeapon` that fires stones as ammo — damage/accuracy become the weapon's own stats (easy, strong) but it exits the throwing system entirely (missile skills, no EB-throw synergy, Gravel Whorl deflects it as physical projectile like any bullet).
- **Visor "highlights targets"**: no native target-highlight part found; the mechanical half (hit more often) is fully covered by close-accuracy+range properties; literal highlight rendering would be bespoke C#.

## Cooking effects  ← opened 2026-07-22 (user request; engine recon DONE, proposal drafted, awaiting rulings)

### Cooking-system ground truth (decompiled + base XML, 2026-07-22)
- **Any item becomes a campfire ingredient by carrying `<part Name="PreparedCookingIngredient" type="X" charges="1" />`.** No food requirement — Phase Silk is the base-game precedent for a non-food ingredient. `Campfire.cs` enumerates inventory purely by presence of the part (plus unsealed liquid containers whose liquid class defines a cooking type — salt→thirst, convalessence→regenLowtier; liquid types live in the C# liquid classes, so mod liquids would define their own). Cooking **consumes** a serving/charge.
- **`type` keywords are data, not code:** each keyword resolves to a blueprint named `ProceduralCookingIngredient_{type}` inheriting `IngredientMapping` (base game: Data.xml) whose tags do all the work — `Units` / `Triggers` / `Actions` (comma-lists of effect class names), `Description` (auto-composed onto the item: "Adds X-based effects to cooked meals."), and `RandomWeight` / `RandomHighTierWeight` (nonzero = the domain joins `random`/`randomHighTier` ingredient rolls, e.g. Crusty Loaf). **New domains are pure XML** pointing at effect classes; the keyword string is arbitrary (`stone` is free).
- **Effect classes are small C#:** passive unit = `ProceduralCookingEffectUnit` (Init/Apply/Remove/GetDescription, ~30 lines — `CookingDomainArmor_UnitAV` is literally "+2 AV"); trigger = `ProceduralCookingEffectWithTrigger` (registers events, calls `Trigger()` — armor's fires on suffering a 2X+ penetration); triggered action = `ProceduralCookingTriggeredAction`. Procedural meals draw units (passive whole-day buff) or trigger+action pairs ("whenever X, Y") from the domains of the chosen ingredients.
- **Grind/preserve step is pure XML:** `<part Name="PreservableItem" Result="{blueprint}" Number="N" />` on a raw item enables the campfire **preserve** action → N product items (Starapple → 5 Starapple Preserves). Rocks could preserve into powder ingredients.
- **66 base domain keywords.** Of note for us: `thirst`, `fungus`, `heat`, `cold`, `electric`, `phase`, `teleport`, `fear` (Dried Lah Petals), `darkness` (Dried Vanta Petals), `love`, `armor` (Compacted Bone Meal — bone→stone adjacency), `tastyMinor`, `density` (**permanent +AV** unit; RandomWeight 0 / HighTier 10 — the base game gates it behind rare ingredients; if we use it, reserve for something genuinely rare).

### Meal-generation ground truth (decompiled 2026-07-22 second dig + wiki cross-check — the "middle of the campfire" gap is CLOSED)
- **Each selected ingredient contributes exactly ONE domain type** (`GetTypeInstanceAvoidingList` — multi-type ingredients carry comma-lists of type options and pick one not already used; `random`/Have-It-All resolves to a random eligible ingredient's domain). **Duplicate domains collapse** (wiki confirms: "adding the same cooking domain twice has no effect") — two heat rocks in one pot = one heat contribution.
- **Meal effect shape = shuffled permutation walk** (`GenerateEffectFromTypeList`): all permutations of the type list + a null sentinel, shuffled, first viable wins — null/1-type ⇒ `CreateJustUnits(ALL types)`; 2-type ⇒ random trigger from domain A + random action from domain B (both must have them); 3-type ⇒ unit from A + trigger from B + action from C. So 2 ingredients roll {both basics} or {whenever-X-then-Y in either order}; 3 ingredients add the base+trigger+action shape.
- **One random unit per domain per meal** (`GetTag("Units").Split(',').GetRandomElement()`), same for triggers/actions; `@`-prefixed entries indirect through a population table (another blend dial, free).
- **The `*Minor` domains are units-only** (no Triggers/Actions tags) — they can never form "whenever X, Y" combos; full domains can. **A native two-tier lever for us.**
- Counts/limits: choose **2 ingredients, 3 with `CookingAndGathering_Spicer`**; cook **up to 3× per hunger cycle** (`CookCount`); must be hungry to cook; **no Cooking and Gathering skill = no ingredient effects at all** (decomp: the unskilled random path rolls `chance = 0`, never adds types — wiki agrees).
- Inspired/Carbide Chef: rolls **3 candidate effects** from the same algorithm, player picks, result is saved + learned as a named recipe (`CookingRecipe.FromIngredients` → `LearnRecipe`). Other recipe sources: water ritual, recipe books (green names), pariah secret trades. Cooking from a recipe = deterministic stored effect, bypasses the roll.
- Tasty: 10% base (+bonus param), **forced by any `tastyMinor` ingredient** — and base-game **salt (the liquid) is already tastyMinor**, so the chunk-of-salt rock tagging is precedent-perfect. Fasting Way / Mind over Body extend metabolizing duration (wiki; not re-verified in code).

## Lithovore (rock-eater defect)  ← opened 2026-07-22; ALL RULINGS LANDED same day; **PASS 1 BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-22** *(the "held until the Fissure verify boot" note is obsolete — the boot cleared same day; deployment confirmed in `Mods\TerraFirma` 2026-07-23, and Lithovore played live in the verification sessions)*

### Build record (2026-07-22)
*(closed items lifted from the live block of the same name; open flags stayed in TODO.md)*

- [x] `[bug]` **Streakschorl flight LEAKED the never-miss accuracy lend — CAUGHT BY LOG-WATCH + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23 (active next restart):** the hijacked flight bypasses `WeaponThrowHit`/`AfterThrownEvent` (the lend's return points) and the stone is spent at flight's end, so the thrower kept `CloseThrowRangeAccuracyBonus 100` **permanently** (evidence: one lend line across three flights — flights 2–3 saw the property already present and skipped re-lending; this was exactly the round-12 "stale lend" caveat, now confirmed real and worse than caveated). Fix: `ReturnLentAccuracy` made public; the flight hijack calls it at commit, before SplitFromStack. **Residual: the current playtest character carries a stale permanent +100 close-throw accuracy** from the pre-fix flights — the fix can't retroactively strip it (the property is indistinguishable from the cybernetic's legit grant). User call: keep the god-arm on this character, or strip it manually next session (one-line wish-script).

### Stone milling — superseded pre-build spec (kept for reference)
User concept: preserving rocks requires carrying a **mortar and pestle**. Rulings landed same day:
1. **Tool = the base game's own Pestle** (verified: Items.xml Tier-0 stone cudgel, weight 1, "dusted by humoric powders," already spawns on apothecary NPCs + loot tables, mechanically jobless — the mod gives it its purpose). No new item.
2. **Powder profile:** weight 0, AlwaysStack, no decay/fragility, not throwable; servings per rock 2–3 (DRAFT).
3. **Powders are NOT edible** — purely cooking carriers ("a preservative on cooking"). Whole rocks stay the lithovore's food.
4. **Domainless rocks grind to plain "rock flour"** — closes the dull-rock loop with a real decision: mill before the 500-turn decay clock or lose the domain (the two systems interlock, zero new code).
5. **Grinding grenade-armed rocks warns** (the dangerous-drink prompt genre), doesn't refuse.
6. **UX RULED: batch menu, not per-stone actions** — user: per-stone grind too tedious; wants the preserve-exotic shape (choose specific stacks + quantities — you may want to keep a particular rock). **Native seam verified:** `GetCookingActionsEvent.SendToActorAndObject(player, campfire)` (Campfire.cs:1291) — a mod part can add "Grind stones" to the campfire menu with NO Harmony; `PreserveExotic()`'s decompiled loop (PickOption + AskNumber per stack) is the UI recipe to mirror.
- **Build plan:** one blueprint `Cleo_TerraFirma_StoneMeal` whose `PreparedCookingIngredient.type` + DisplayName are **stamped at grind time from the source rock** (parts are instance data — verify the runtime stamp at build; if it fails, fall back to per-rock powder blueprints + maintenance rule). One small part carrying the menu handler + grind loop. **Open wiring question for build:** where the handler lives — on the Pestle blueprint via Merges.xml *if* GetCookingActionsEvent cascades into inventory (check its CascadeLevel), else on the player (Lithovore part hosts it pass 1, generalized later — but grinding should NOT be lithovore-gated; any cook with a pestle mills).
- **DRAFT numbers awaiting sign-off:** 2–3 servings per rock; boulders grindable for more (5–6?); energy per grind ~1000; campfire required (it's where the menu lives); Cooking and Gathering required (base preserve parity) — flag if the skill gate should be dropped in favor of pestle-only.


### Engine ground truth (decompiled 2026-07-22 — fresh full dump in session scratchpad `decomp/`)
- **Defect declaration is pure XML:** base `Mutations.xml` uses `<category Name="PhysicalDefects" ... Defect="true">` with **negative Cost** entries (`Amphibious Cost="-3"`, `Carnivorous Cost="-2"`), `MaxSelected="1"`, `BearerDescription`, Tile. The mod's Mutations.xml already merges into base categories by name (Physical/Mental) — same pattern, new entry under `PhysicalDefects` with `Cost="-3"`.
- **Carnivorous (the base "you can only eat X" defect) is a hollow marker class** — every behavior is a hardcoded `HasPart<Carnivorous>()` check scattered across `Food.cs` (satiation gate, Gross-food bypass, IllnessChance 50% raw non-meat), `Stomach.cs` (`"AddFood"` string-event handler), and `Campfire.cs` (plant/fungus ingredient + recipe blocks, 50% Ill meal path). **A mod defect can ride none of it** — we build our own gates. All hooks verified native:
  - *Block normal food:* defect part handles `BeforeConsumeEvent` — verified dispatched to the **Actor** first (`IConsumeEvent.DispatchAll`), `Eat`/`Drink` flags distinguish channels, and `Food.cs` checks it before any satiation/heal lands; returning false cancels cleanly. Gate `Eat` only — drinking untouched.
  - *Eat rocks:* defect part handles `OwnerGetInventoryActionsEvent` (verified, `IInventoryActionsEvent`) → adds an "Eat" action to `Cleo_TerraFirma_IsAnyStone`-tagged items. **No Food part on rocks** — nobody without the defect can eat them, and no part-order collisions with grenade-armed rocks. On command: fire `"AddFood" Satiation=Snack` at self (Stomach handles natively; honey/algae/cider liquids are the precedent callers), apply the bonus effect, `UseEnergy(1000)`, split one off the stack and destroy (Food.cs's own sequence).
  - *Bonus effect:* `FirePayload(eater, eater, ...)` already exists (attacker param threaded for lovestone) — reusable where self-targeting makes sense; needs per-rock judgment (eating grimslate to terrify yourself is comedy, not a bonus).
- **The one wall: cooked meals.** Campfire's eat-a-meal path is direct method calls (`ClearHunger()` at 4 sites, recipe effects via `ApplyEffectsTo`) with the Carnivorous restriction hardcoded inline — no event a mod part can catch. Any meal restriction for the rock-eater = **Harmony** (policy: avoid-preferred, not strict; the Scanning postfix is the surviving precedent).

### Forks to rule (one at a time, per house style)
1. **Meal policy — RULED 2026-07-22: C, reframed as an ingredient-class ban (the Carnivorous mirror inverted).** Lithovores are banned from cooking with **plants and meats**; **stones and ALL liquids are legal**, and by class-ban logic so are the exotic ingredients that are neither (bone meal, mirror dust, spark tick plasma, dream smoke, congealed love...). Mechanical mirror of Carnivorous, per decompiled shape: banned ingredients are **hidden from the lithovore's ingredient picker** (Campfire.cs:813 pattern — can't transgress by accident), while preset oven meals and learned recipes containing a banned class hit the punishment path (no nourishment + 50% Ill, Campfire.cs:743 pattern). Harmony sites: the picker filter + preset-meal eat + the two recipe-cook paths (the four call sites already mapped). Detection: `HasTag("Plant")`/`HasTag("Meat")` on ingredients; recipes via `HasPlants()` + a meat equivalent (verify `HasMeat()` exists or compute from stored blueprints at build). **Both clarifications RATIFIED 2026-07-22:** (a) fungus stays legal — mushrooms are honorary minerals for lithovores (they grow in caves; the fungal rocks agree); (b) plain no-ingredient meals nourish (lenient falls out of class-ban logic; accepted).
2. **Eaten-effect model — DIRECTION RULED (user, 2026-07-22): reuse base cooking effects wherever possible** (soupstone = weak hydration, sludgestone = weak slime, chunk of salt = always tasty), with an "inedible/dangerous to eat" section. **Full draft table delivered: `design/rock-eaten-effects.md`** — all catalog entries mapped to verified base classes (5 sections: buff reuse / gas-rock "burp family" / plain rations / dangerous / structurally inedible) + 5 sub-forks awaiting rulings (strength dial, burp-family ratification, danger policy, deep-four risk-reward, strange-rock recursion). Key engine wins recorded there: `ProceduralCookingEffect.CreateSpecific(units)` is a public static factory taking class-name strings (mod classes resolve too), and both cooking-effect families natively expire on `BecameHungry`/`ClearFoodEffects` — eaten-effects follow food rules (until next hunger, one at a time) with zero duration code.
3. **What counts as a rock:** all `IsAnyStone` (Small Stones become trail rations; you can eat your own EB pebbles — funny, probably fine) vs fissure rocks only (tighter, needs its own tag check).
4. **Cost sanity check (user's number either way):** Carnivorous is **−2** for a harsher day-to-day restriction with zero upside; −3 for a defect that Fissure trivializes (food literally rains from the ceiling) *plus* eat-bonuses is generous unless fork 1 lands on (B)/(C). Counterweights that are real: stone weight vs food weight, and losing the cooking metagame under (B)/(C).
5. **Exclusions:** `Carnivorous` (the combo breaks our AddFood path: their handler eats the satiation and rolls 50% Ill on non-meat) and likely `Photosynthetic Skin` (the base food-economy pair already excludes Carnivorous both ways).
- **Free wins, no rulings needed:** plain fallen rock is decay-exempt (`DullAfter=0`) → the natural shelf-stable ration, finally giving dull rocks the use the cooking section wanted; hoard-or-hurl grows its third verb (*or swallow*) organically; fragility never interacts (eating consumes outright, no HoldChance roll).
- **Ruling round 3 (2026-07-22) — the defect's basics, all RULED:**
  - **Name: Lithovore.** BearerDescription proposed default "lithovores" (flag for text pass).
  - **Cost: 2 MP** (`Cost="-2"`) — Carnivorous parity; user had misremembered Carnivorous at 3.
  - **Scope: all `IsAnyStone` PLUS gemstones.** Gemstone eaten-effect deferred to edibility passes 2/3 — user concept EARMARKED there: Ego bonus is blocked (lovestone owns Ego), so instead possibly tie to the relic-style transmute-enemy-into-gemstone effect as a serious cost/value proposition.
  - **Exclusions: Carnivorous + Photosynthetic Skin** (mirrors the base PS↔Carnivorous pair).
  - **Danger policy: eatable with warning** — condition verified in decomp: base dangerous drinks prompt `Popup.WarnYesNoCancel("Are you sure you want to drink that?")` behind `Options.ConfirmDangerousLiquid` (LiquidVolume.cs:3138). Dangerous rock eats mirror the same prompt + option gate.
  - **Deep four eaten: pure danger RULED, plus approved direction — "powerful elemental pulse" on eat** (fire the stone's elemental payload centered on the eater; numbers at build).
  - **Statue-eating: living Stoneshaper statues COUNT** — "eating your buddy because you're starving makes thematic sense"; implementation shape (deanimate-then-consume vs direct) decided at build.
  - **Strange rock RULED (round 4):** cooked = one random domain **from the rock ingredient set** (comma option list of every assigned rock domain — native pick-one mechanism; maintenance rule: the list grows with the table), NOT Have-It-All's global `random`. Eaten raw = roll only rocks whose eaten entry actually does something (no fizzles). Always-warn stands. **User also reaffirmed: the strange rock's mimic table needs a cleanup pass at some point — "some of the things it has don't really make sense"; adds mandate to the held strange-stone audit (see Fissure section).**
- **Ruling round 2 (2026-07-22):** raw-eat model re-based on base raw-ingredient behavior — verified: base preserved ingredients eaten raw do NOTHING (`Preservable` = `Food Satiation="None"`), and base expresses raw-eat effects through a family of twenty stock `*OnEat` parts (Dreadroot's `FearOnEat 3d8/4d6` is the magnitude register). Eaten-effects become stock OnEat parts at snack-grade dice; the defect's eat channel replays Food's own event sequence so they fire natively; rocks carry no live Food part. Full findings + pure-XML mappings in `design/rock-eaten-effects.md` § "Ruling round 2". **Liquids stay legal cooking partners for lithovores** (constrains the meal-policy fork: whatever shape lands, liquid ingredients don't get blocked). **Statue-eating RULED as concept: famished lithovores can eat statues, the way carnivorous creatures eat corpses.** Open details, user designs: which statues qualify (base `Statue`/`RandomStatue` furniture; the mod's inert container statues — eating one should spill the entombed gear via the existing `ReturnPossessions` helper; gorgon-victim statues = eating the petrified, maximally Qud); mechanism (statues are world objects, not items — bespoke "devour" interaction on an adjacent statue from the defect part, famished-gated exactly like base Gross food, `HungerLevel >= 2`); satiation value and whole-vs-bites.
- **Extensions floated ("and more…"), parked unruled:** stonecarver NPCs carrying the defect (they snack on the quarry — free lore texture for the Quarriers workstream); wall-growth ingredients feeding the same pantry; the stone-soup named recipe (Tier 3 stretch) as the defect's signature meal.

## Pending design budget (ideas parked, not committed)

### Creature/NPC groundwork (recon 2026-07-22 — all decompiled ground truth, ready for the creatures workstream)
- **No mod mutation advertises to NPC brains except Fissure** (got its `AIGetOffensiveAbilityListEvent` handler in the rebuild). The pattern is ~10 lines: handle the event, `E.Add(COMMAND_ID)` gated on `E.Distance` / LOS / `IsMyActivatedAbilityAIUsable` — Cryokinesis, Sunder Mind, and Burgeoning are clean templates.
- **AI-fired commands carry the target**: `AICommandList.HandleCommandList` → `CommandEvent.Send(Owner, Command, Target, ...)`, so `E.Target` is populated in `CommandEvent` handlers. Player-only `PickDestinationCell` paths (Gorgon's Gaze, Stoneshaper) need an NPC branch that reads `E.Target` instead.
- **NPC channel maintenance** (for wild gorgons): Sunder Mind's caster-side `MemberOfPsychicBattle` effect forces `PassTurn()` in `BeforeTakeActionEvent` for non-player casters — mirror that in Gorgon's `BeginTakeActionEvent` (tick first, then pass). Note: `TickGaze` currently only breaks on zone-exit; a per-tick LOS-break dial was discussed 2026-07-22 and left **unruled**.
- **Blueprint `<mutation Name="X">` resolves by CLASS name** (`XRL.World.Parts.Mutation.X`, verified in GameObjectFactory) — creatures use `<mutation Name="Cleo_TerraFirma_GorgonsGaze" Level="4" />`, not the display name.
- **Save math**: defender rolls d20 + stat mod vs (base DC + attacker's AttackerStat mod). Creature Willpower is the gorgon difficulty dial: +1 DC per 2 points above 16.
- **Benchmarks**: Lithofex — L35, 175 HP, AV 12, Artillery role, one-save-or-die DC 20+Ego gaze, range 7+Lv after 3-turn telegraph. Cragmensch Boulderer — `FabricateFromSelf ... AIUseForThrowing="true"` is **native AI rock-throwing** (carves boulders out of its own HP): the zero-new-code path for wild throwers; the mod's daze part is blueprint-attachable for a wild-pebble variant.
- **Calcify NPC gap** (parked above, still open): handler is player-only cell-picking (`Mutation_Calcify.cs:65`), no AI advertisement — wild calcifiers need both built.
- **Nothing is designed.** Creature sketches floated in the 2026-07-22 session (gorgon ~L18–20 Artillery, defensive calcifier, Boulderer-pattern slinger) were reconnaissance only — user designs creatures collaboratively; no concepts, names, stats, or descriptions are committed.
- Side note: Burrowing Urchin likely already joins dynamic urchin encounter pools (inherits `BaseUrchin`, no `ExcludeFromDynamicEncounters`) — answers the old companion `[verify]` item pending in-game confirmation.

- **Modular disable options (user, 2026-07-24)** — an options menu where any mutation from the set can be individually disabled, plus (potentially) one switch to turn off all mod-added stones. Not now; park until wanted. **Mechanism RECON DONE 2026-07-24 (decomp + a friend's recipe):** the key insight (friend's, premise decomp-CONFIRMED): creature blueprints resolve mutations by C# CLASS name, never through Mutations.xml — so hiding the Mutations.xml entry disables chargen/mutation-buy while every creature keeps working. The engine primitives (MutationFactory decomp): `Hidden` attribute at file/category/entry level (the loader's own deprecation note says "set Hidden attribute instead" of removal), entries MERGE BY NAME across files, and `ExcludeFromPool` covers random-mutation rolls. The friend's two-file version (Mutations.xml + HiddenMutations.xml switched per option) needs an option-conditional file-load facility we could NOT confirm in decomp; the VERIFIED route is the engine's own Prerelease gate pattern (MutationFactory.HandleMutationNode: `if (Prerelease && !Options.EnablePrereleaseContent) { Hidden = true; ExcludeFromPool = true; }`) — a small mod hook post-Init applies the same two flags per our options. Options changes take effect on restart (factory inits once). Stones switch: option check at the Fissure roll + table entry points. Save-compat rule still owed once public: disabling mid-save orphans existing mutants/objects.
- **Visible "crumbling" status on timed falls (friend suggestion via user, 2026-07-24)** — the mechanic itself is ALREADY BUILT (`Part_TimedForm`, same day, convergent design: ape → rubble, baboon → figurine); the flag-worthy delta is presentation: a visible debuff/effect on the creature ("crumbling") so the player can see it's temporary, instead of the current silent countdown. Optional polish; a small Effect costume over the existing part if wanted.
- **"Can buy, can't sell" on fissure stones (friend suggestion via user, 2026-07-24)** — economy guard against infinite-Fissure gold farming. Probably unnecessary, as the user's own framing notes: rocks decay (DullAfter), base Commerce is 1 dram (selling spam is beggar's work), and Burgeoning's unlimited harvestables are the tolerated precedent. Park; if ever wanted, recon whether the engine has a clean no-sell facility (none known offhand) vs Commerce 0.
- **Real wall-phasing on phasalt (SLIM POTENTIAL — user 2026-07-25: "agree, put it in design budget as a slim potential")** — the deliberate successor to the gated no-op (the Effect/Phased payload now withholds itself from walls, 2026-07-25): a phasalt wall-hit that genuinely opens the wall for phase-walking. Would need real design (whose phase changes, duration, render, doorway abuse) plus decomp of whether a Phased wall actually admits normal walkers — the user's 8-throw test suggests the engine never manifests it. Slim by their word; park until wanted.
- **Geode family (user, 2026-07-23)** — "potentially adding geodes": broaden beyond the existing brine geode (né soupstone) into a crack-open family. Natural hooks: gemstone-bearing geodes (Geomancy scan synergy + the transmute-gemstone earmark), crystal/mineral yields feeding the kitchen, the crush/"Detonate reads as crush" action as the opening verb. Undesigned; park until wanted.
- **Pour option on the liquid rocks (user, 2026-07-23)** — brine geode / sludgestone / oilstone (when built) should be emptiable into containers. Two shapes at build: (a) give the rocks a real `LiquidVolume` — native Pour/Fill interactions free, but adds drink/weight semantics and interacts with the Pass 2 **pool-volume audit** (brine geode's 500-dram "pool" render — decide the dram budget once, both features use it); (b) bespoke "empty" inventory action minting N drams into a chosen container, rock crumbles/dulls after. Claude leans (a) if the audit lands on sane volumes.
- **Creatures that spawn with these mutations innately** — wild gorgons, calcifying beasts, etc. *(Gorgon's Willpower-scaled DC makes creature Willpower the difficulty knob.)*
- **Flavor items + worldbuilding** — books, relics, lore hooks tying the mutations into Qud.
- **Wall kick** — as an item, skill, or mutation; form undecided.
- **Retrofitting mutations onto one-time-use items** — won't fit most of them, but Geomancy is a good candidate (a consumable "throw this stone, reveal the area" item).

## Automated verification harness  ← NEW WORKSTREAM (user-blessed 2026-07-24: "i'm pro building this but mostly because i'm curious if it will actually work out")

### Decomp-verified foundations (all 2026-07-24, game 2.0.211.50)
- **Player-path throws**: `GameObject.PerformThrow` is public and fires `BeforeThrown` internally
  (GameObject.cs:15333) — a direct call IS the full player pipeline. On the streakschorl hijack
  path (handler returns false) PerformThrow exits BEFORE the engine's own protected
  `EquipReplacementThrownWeapon` (15340 vs 15344), so the mod's replicated flow is provably the
  only re-equip in play — the scenario tests exactly the re-fix.
- **Turn pump**: the Paralyzed idiom — `BeginTakeActionEvent` + `E.PreventAction = true` on a
  player-attached part; the engine zeroes the actor's energy itself (ActionManager.cs:743-746).
  World runs at full AI while the player provably does nothing; immune to AutoAct
  hostile-interrupts, no UI involvement.
- **Popups**: `XRL.UI.Popup.Suppress` (static bool) around scenario actions.
- **Force-powering a wish-spawned mill**: `Mill : IPoweredPart`, so `ChargeUse = 0` = trivially
  powered, unpayable ChargeUse = guaranteed dead — both hopper branches forced without a power grid.
- **Stack trap dodged (memory note "slot-stack remainders mimic bag re-equips")**: streak setup
  equips ONE stone BEFORE granting spares — three-at-once would merge into an equipped stack and
  the flight's stack-split would fake the pass.

### PoC scenarios (the current batch's riskiest items)
- `tfstreak` — synchronous: spawns a baboon on a 3-cell lane, equips 1 streakschorl + 2 bag
  spares, `PerformThrow`, asserts the slot re-armed with a DIFFERENT streakschorl object.
- `tfhopper` — 5 turns: two spawned mills (force-live/force-dead), 2 salt rocks each; asserts
  live mill = 0 stones + ≥4 powders, dead mill = 2 stones + 0 powders. Tick-1 line logs both
  mills' `IsReady` so a force-power failure names itself as a harness artifact, not a mod bug.
- `tfscav` — 30 turns: 4 spawned baboons (3 Generic stones each) vs the standing player;
  per-baboon counter parts sample the thrown slot every baboon-turn ((ref,count) shrink/swap =
  throw, cap 1/turn; slot EMPTY + stones pocketed = STARVED turn — the cadence regression's
  in-code H1 signature). PASS = 0 starved + >0 throws; cadence REPORTED not asserted (≥vanilla
  still needs the user's control-run baseline). NOTE: the player eats 30 turns of baboon
  throws — run on a character that can shrug it off.
- `tfverify` — help + kill-switch status.
- Housekeeping: spawns stamped `TFHarnessSpawn=1`, swept (Obliterate) at scenario finish;
  mid-scenario save/load aborts as INCONCLUSIVE (live state is deliberately non-serialized)
  instead of asserting against reloaded ghosts.

**→ THE LEDGER: `design/harness-verified.md`** (user request 2026-07-24: "keep a tab on anything
solved that way so if things go funny in the future, we know where to look") — one entry per
harness-proven fact with the exact assertion, the explicit NOT-covered limits, and the re-run
wish. If a listed behavior goes funny later: re-run the wish first; a flipped verdict localizes
the regression, an unchanged PASS points elsewhere. Maintain on every run that proves something.

### First live run — 2026-07-24, same evening (user: "i got pass, pass, fail, but on retrying 1, only fail")
**Verdict: the harness works.** Two scenario PASSes closed real batch verifies on first contact;
the two failures were both HARNESS bugs, both diagnosed from the log in minutes, both fixed +
redeployed same evening (fixes active next restart):
- [x] **tfstreak run 1 PASS** — "slot after flight = Hypersonic x1, bag 2->1 (re-equipped from
  bag, player path)". **Batch item 1 (re-equip re-fix) VERIFIED by direct engine-state assertion**
  — sheet updated. Remaining streakschorl tails (whorl-vs-flight, render modes, save/load) unchanged.
- [x] **tfhopper PASS** — tick-1 readiness line proved the ChargeUse forcing (live=True dead=False);
  powered mill: 2 stones -> 5 powders in 5 turns, dead mill inert. **Batch item 4's MECHANISM
  verified**; Joppa's real river-powered mill still gets an eyeball for the world-wiring half.
- [x] **tfscav "FAIL" = harness sampling artifact, and the data inside it is a PASS for batch
  item 3:** all 8 "starved" turns were phantoms — the counter samples before the scavenger's
  same-turn free re-ready; the log shows every slot->EMPTY answered by "pocket re-ready ...
  (free action)" within the turn, **ZERO REFUSED lines (H1's smoking gun stayed silent)**, plus
  the dry-fetch loop working live (detour d=5-8 -> stoop -> re-armed). Counter FIXED: starved
  now = EMPTY on two consecutive samples (emptiness that survives a whole turn). Cadence
  measured 0.29 throws/baboon-turn over 118 baboon-turns — the ≥vanilla comparison still owes
  the control baseline (vanilla can't run under the mod's global merges; Tier 2 territory or a
  mod-disabled boot). Bonus find: baboons carry native Small Stones alongside the granted rocks
  and throw both — pockets read 6 at spawn, not 3.
- [x] **tfstreak retry SETUP FAIL = harness stacking bug, FIXED:** run 1's re-equipped stone
  still occupied the slot; the retry's new stone stacked into the leftovers and the held ref
  went stale ("could not equip"). Fix: normalize the slot (`BodyPart.ForceUnequip`) then equip
  by `ForceEquipObject` — never transits the bag (both APIs decomp-verified).

Six new scenarios + a key discovery-by-recon. The forced-crossing trick (`DirectMoveTo` forced

### The miss saga — SOLVED 2026-07-24 late night (watcher window + decomp; user hypothesis "it's just not penetrating?" ruled out honorably)
The watcher caught a repeat batch run on the old build and the cage throw's own lines told it:
"throw resolved: at rest; hit-payload fired=False -> miss: payload fires at the landing cell."
**ALL the round-3 throw failures (cage 3/8, drain flesh-in-batch, drain robot both runs) were
plain MISSES.** The chain of facts, each decomp- or XML-verified:
- The user's penetration theory was reasonable but the engine clears it: `WeaponThrowHit` fires
  on any CONNECTED hit regardless of penetrations (GameObject.cs:15612 — zero pens still
  delivers the Defender), so a hit-no-pen robot would still latch. The robot never connected.
- **Only streakschorl is NeverMiss** (one blueprint, Rocks.xml:633) — the catalog throws
  honestly: AimVariance scatter + the engine's fall-one-short distance variance. Misses firing
  the payload at the landing cell is WORKING AS DESIGNED mod-side.
- The verify marathons never surfaced this because **the playtest god-arm (+100
  CloseThrowRangeAccuracyBonus) masked the whole layer** (GameObject.cs:15389: the property
  zeroes AimVariance AND skips the fall-short branch) — the stale-accuracy cleanup item just
  earned its keep as an accidental test-fixture confound.
- **Harness bug #5, FIXED + DEPLOYED: ThrowAt assumed guaranteed hits.** Now it uses the mod's
  own streakschorl lend idiom — stamp the accuracy property for the throw, return it after
  (mirrors `ReturnLentAccuracy`, RemoveIfZero and all). Deterministic hits, no god-arm needed.
- Residual: drainstone-vs-robot is DOWNGRADED from bug-candidate to "probably just missed
  twice"; next tfdrain (lend + victim-named diagnostics) closes it either way.

### Fungal lottery — diagnosis SHARPENED 2026-07-24 late night (same watcher window)
Three SPORE lines in one boot: first fungal-rock creation "ROLLED FRESH - stored state was
empty" (rolled 1203, stored), next two "loaded from game state" (1203, 1203). So persistence
works ALL SESSION; the stored key is gone at the NEXT boot's first access. Exactly two suspects
left, and tonight's data cannot split them: **(a)** `SetStringGameState` genuinely not surviving
save/quit/load, or **(b)** the harness-iteration sessions END WITHOUT SAVING (quit-without-save /
killed window loses the write legitimately — the code would be innocent). **The 30-second
decider, user's hands:** note the permutation, SAVE AND QUIT properly, reload, mint any fungal
rock (a tfstrange roll usually does it), compare. Same = (b), close the bug; different = (a),
design real storage. Do this BEFORE any redesign.
**DECIDED 2026-07-25 via the tfspore pure-read wish (user: "all 3 tests gave back 3201, looks
good"): (b) CONFIRMED — 3201 held across mint, save-quit, and reload. The lottery bug is
CLOSED as not-a-bug; the storage design was right all along. Ledger entry added.**

### Round 4 first run — 2026-07-24/25 boundary: 7 for 7 on the new scenarios
**shape.carve, shape.ivory (wall intact, cooldown untouched), shape.swap, shape.cd (1200→1171,
pure tick — the retirement watch is a measurement now), puddle (first throw), devour (EaterStatue
whole, hunger restored) — all PASS**; the SETUP SKIPs behaved (shape skipped a non-Stoneshaper,
devour a non-lithovore, both later re-run green on the right character). Sheet: shape-cd →
pass, shape-ivory strengthened, lith-devour + rock-onepuddle written. **LOTTERY NOTE:** this

Root cause: the fractured-plates build probed NEW tile variants (SpriteManager.TryGetTextureInfo) DURING enqueueing -- the probe's miss path loads the texture under the atlas WRITE lock, and the Unity thread was concurrently fetching our already-enqueued sprites under the same lock: deadlock. Probabilistic interleave (casts 1-2 were cache-warm or lucky; cast 3 hit a fresh mask with sprites in flight). Fix: PLAN-before-PLAY -- plates computed first, every needed variant resolved while the juice queue is quiet, enqueue-time lookups are pure cache hits (CutPlates hoisted so the same rolled plate lists feed warmup and enqueue). DEFINITIVE FIX 2026-08-04: PROBING DELETED ENTIRELY.** Tile paths are now COMPOSED the zone painter's own way (PaintedWallAtlas prefix + paint + 8-digit suffix + extension, per-material tags read from the sampled wall / blueprint, painter defaults otherwise) with zero atlas access from the game thread -- textures resolve Unity-side inside GetPooledSprite exactly like base-game juice. A variant the atlas doesn't stock renders as the engine's blank InvalidInfo with a one-time "No texture found by ID" warning in Player.log: annoying, never fatal, and the warning names the missing path. ~~`[verify]` cast fissure + calcify on a fresh boot (the cold-cache case both freezes hit)~~ **COLD-CACHE CAST GREEN 2026-08-06 residue boot (user: "booted for 2, no crash/freeze") — the probe-deletion fix holds on the exact case both freezes hit.** Still cheap and standing: grep Player.log for "No texture found" after a play session to catch any composed path the atlas lacks (benign, names the path).

The flat block was OUR OWN HELD SPRITE: stayUp rim/calcify sprites held opaquely at full emergence, pixel-aligned to the grid, until the juice manager's `finish()` -- which runs on TURN cadence (`clearUpToTurn`) and can lag well past the handoff. A lagging opaque sprite over the correct wall = a flat stone block wearing chain-mask tiles, "healing into full form" at the sweep. Colors were never involved. **Fix: stayUp retired -- every sprite sinks after its hold (behind the freshly landed wall, no visible dip) and once fully sunk PARKS ITSELF behind the map (the pre-delay z+100 idiom) -- sprite exit is now unconditional, independent of finish() timing.** `[verify]` recast both mutations: walls should wear proper painted faces from the moment each lands, no flat impostors lingering; the wall-landed log lines stay in for this boot.

One refinement from the proposal: `[ModSensitiveCacheInit]` DROPPED deliberately — it fires inside the cache RESET, where forcing `MutationFactory.CheckInit()` could parse XML earlier than the engine intends; shipped seams are `[PreGameCacheInit]` (XRLCore startup + every `NewGame()` → `LoadEverything()`, decomp-verified to run BEFORE chargen — so no-restart option flips are confirmed engine behavior, not hope) + `[CallAfterGameLoaded]` (loaded saves' mid-run buys see in-session flips). Off-check is an explicit `"No"` — missing/unset options leave the mutation live. Watch for side effects on gemstone trade interactions. **RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-25 (user: "we would have to add the behavior for them to be thrown slot, but i think that's a good idea. i think default equip slot is XML anyways"):** recon confirmed base `Gemstone` (Items.xml:11599, BaseCurrency child) carries no ThrownWeapon part — that part is what routes default equip to the Thrown Weapon slot; the Gemstone merge now adds `<part Name="ThrownWeapon" Damage="1d2" />` (Small Stone parity, DRAFT for ratify). Watch items: creatures holding gems can now ready and throw them; trade interactions. `[verify]` equip a rough gemstone → lands in the thrown slot.
- [ ] `[bug]` **Baboons never scavenge rocks (user flag 2026-07-24: "baboons don't appear to scavenge for rocks. we need to do some experimentation on that")** — `[SCAV]` fetch has never fired across the whole session despite baboons and loose stones sharing zones constantly. Recon findings: baboons inherit **Humanoid** (Thrown Weapon slot EXISTS — that gate is fine) and carry base **`AIThrowAndScoot`**; `TryScavenge` has four silent early-return gates. **Prime suspect: the `HasGoal("MoveTo")` gate vs the scoot loop** — if scooting keeps a MoveTo goal alive, the scavenger starves forever; second suspect: pocket stones never run dry (does AIThrowAndScoot actually throw them?). **Experiment kit DEPLOYED (diagnostics only, per the flag-don't-fix rule — behavior unchanged):** sparse gate snapshots (1-in-50 ticks per creature) name which gate blocks. Protocol next restart: (1) idle test — wish a plain `Baboon` away from hostiles, scatter stones within 8 cells, wait ~50 turns, read the gate lines; (2) ammo test — provoke a Hulking Baboon at range, watch whether it throws its pockets dry, then whether fetch fires; (3) note whether MoveTo-gate lines dominate during scoot. Fix shapes on the shelf pending the verdict: exempt OUR own fetch-goal-free wander from the MoveTo gate / narrow the gate to combat pathing only / teach the scavenger to also stash-to-pockets.
- [ ] **Baboon theories RECONNED (user, 2026-07-24: "1) baboons only throw 1 stone, not their full inventory 2) baboons inventories are being replaced instead of merged, so they have only mod stones"):** **Theory 2 FALSE** — base `Baboon` carries `Small Stone ×5` and the live trail shows both base Small Stone pockets AND mod rocks on the same baboons simultaneously; `Load="Merge"` inventoryobject lines are additive, confirmed by observation. **Theory 1 essentially TRUE, and it's BASE-game behavior:** decompiled `AIThrowAndScoot` only pushes a 1d3 `Flee` goal after a throw — it never initiates throws or re-readies; throwing is the brain's generic equipped-thrown-weapon attack, and nothing re-arms the slot promptly after a throw, so bite becomes the default once adjacent. The re-readies in the trail were opportunistic brain re-equips, not a cycle. (Also explains the scoot-gate mystery: Flee ≠ MoveTo, so our gate never saw it.) **FLAGGED FOR THE SCAV FIXING PASS (user 2026-07-24: "flag theory ones solution as something for the scav fixing pass"):** extend `StoneScavenger` to also re-ready from pockets when the slot is empty (call `Brain.PerformReequip` in the still-has-pocket-ammo branch) — the part's job generalizes from "fetch when dry" to "keep the thrower throwing." Build alongside whatever the dry-fetch verdict demands; one pass covers both halves of the thrower-uptime problem.
  **ESCALATION — VANILLA CONTROL RUN (user 2026-07-24: "test from base game confirms - baboons throw more stones without mods than with"):** the mod is suppressing baboon throw cadence BELOW vanilla baseline — this is no longer "base behavior we might improve" but a regression to find. Mechanism candidates for the pass, in suspicion order: **(H1) mixed-inventory re-ready failure** — baboon packs now hold mod rocks alongside Small Stones; if the brain's `PerformReequip` pick lands on a mod rock it then declines to ready/throw, the re-ready may abort and strand the slot empty (matches the long slot-EMPTY-with-full-pockets stretches in the transition trail); **(H2) the global stone merges** — `StoneMill` part + IsAnyStone tag now sit on EVERY stone including base Small Stones; an added part may change the AI's equip/throw scoring for the ammo itself; **(H3) StoneScavenger's own BeforeTakeAction presence** (passive on paper, but it's the part we added to the brain's turn). Differential experiments: vanilla + ONLY the scavenger merge (isolates H3); mod minus pocket merges (isolates H1); watch which rocks armed baboons actually throw vs sit on (H1/H2 tell). The transition trail already instruments the observation side. Fix must restore ≥vanilla cadence, not just add the pocket re-ready on top of a broken pick. **CLOSURE DATUM (2026-07-24, session's end): the fetch itself FIRED live** — a wild Baboon reached true dry (slot EMPTY, pockets 0) and the scavenger detoured to a loose stone at d=1 with the MoveTo push visible in the trail. The part's own logic is proven sound; the pass's work is purely the cadence regression + making the fetch reachable more often.
- [ ] **Scavenger experiment interim (2026-07-24, snapshots across two windows):** dominant gate is "**slot already armed**" — and not just pockets: plain wild Baboons are armed with MOD rocks they picked up themselves (pebbles/generic/pointy/salt/drifting across the population), so rocks flow into baboon hands fine without our part. Second gate: "still has pocket ammo (Small Stone)" (Hulking, correct stand-down). MoveTo/scoot gate: seen ONCE — not the starver. **Emerging picture: the fetch is a rare-tail behavior because baboons are almost never both unarmed AND empty — the user-visible "they don't scavenge" is mostly the armed steady-state. The decisive observation still owed: one baboon throwing itself completely dry, then the gate lines around it.** (Bonus sighting: a Hulking Baboon stepped on drifting rock and got Phased 15 — the reduced step proc working on NPCs.)

### Gorgon's Gaze — Open bugs / verifications

- [x] `[bug]` **MAJOR: gazing a wall = endless-pass LOCKOUT — DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-08-07 (user report from live play, "gorgon's gaze a holographic shale wall ... endless cycle of passing, not a hard freeze"; compiles green, hash-verified, in tfbatch).** Root cause chain (decomp-verified): the player-path picker's `GetCombatTarget(ParentObject, true)` rides the engine default **`AllowInanimate=true`**, so ANY plain wall or furniture is a legal gaze victim — the holographic part was incidental (its invulnerability never even engages). A brainless Target then NREs `TickGaze`'s `Target.Brain.Attacked` on EVERY BeginTakeActionEvent; the engine aborts the player's action each segment, and the gaze can never break because the break rides UseEnergyEvent — an aborted action never spends energy. Same signature class as the 07-24 TurnTick NRE storm: game live, player locked. **The user's animated-wall theory checked and inverted: an ANIMATED wall is a full creature (Brain + donor stat block, and AnimateEvent strips HologramInvulnerability) — it duels and resolves fine; only PLAIN inanimates lock.** Fix, two layers: (1) command gate — brainless victims refused before the energy/cooldown spend, both player path (ShowFail "You cannot transfix {it}." — DRAFT) and AI path (silent invalid); every true creature in Qud carries a Brain, mindless included, so nothing legit is excluded; (2) TickGaze belt — a brainless Target releases silently instead of NRE-ing, which also **SELF-HEALS any save made inside the lockout** (first tick after load releases the gaze). Harness: `feedback.gazewall` drives BeginGaze→TickGaze against a spawned HolographicShaleWall and asserts release-not-throw. `[verify]` via **`tfgazewall`** (user test design, one-wish staging: plain + animated holo wall side by side) — **LIVE RUN 2026-08-07 morning boot, log-confirmed: the ANIMATED wall duel ran end-to-end** (saves rolled with 2 interleaved successes, DC escalated 12→21 per the +1/fail law, band FX tracked net 0→7, 10th cumulative fail → PETRIFIED, statue + entombed possessions, zero exceptions in the log) **and `feedback.gazewall` PASS same boot** (brainless release belt fired, no NRE). **PLAIN wall refusal CONFIRMED same boot (user: "the holoshale wall refused me so that's good") — the gaze-wall fix is FULLY GREEN:** gate ✓ duel ✓ belt ✓. Residual (only if a locked save ever surfaces): the self-heal ride is code-identical to the harness belt, expected to just work.

### Earthen Barrage — Design calls

- [x] `[design]` ~~Balance pass~~ — done 2026-07-21 vs Light Manipulation (decompiled ground truth). Verdict: EB out-bursts LM ~2:1 vs soft/medium armor with a bigger magazine and daze; falls flat on reliability (to-hit + ~10 tile range vs LM's auto-hit infinite line), out-of-combat utility, and sustain. **Cost stays 4 MP.** *(daze half superseded by the 2026-08-06 cut above)*
- [x] `[design]` ~~Charge regen~~ — **implemented: Willpower-scaled**, Light Manipulation's exact formula (base 15 turns, −5%/point above WIL 16, clamped [3, 75]). Second data point in the mod's "the earth rewards the disciplined" Willpower theme (with Gorgon's Gaze DC).
- [x] `[design]` ~~Projectile protection~~ — **implemented: "Gravel Whorl"** (user design, `Effect_GravelWhorl.cs`). While toggled on with stones in reserve: deflect chance vs *physical* projectiles = 3% × current charges + 1% × level (46% at Lv10 full reserve); lasers/plasma/electric pass through (beam attacks bypass inherently — they never fire `DefenderMissileHitEvent`; energy *weapons* filtered by projectile attributes); +1 AV at 3+ stones, +2 at 9+. Deflecting costs nothing — **throwing depletes the shield**; hoard-or-hurl is the tension. Modeled on Calcify's `WhorlingStones` code shape.
- [x] `[bug]` **Resonant penetration double-dip (user playtest find 2026-07-22, RULED −5):** the level-scaled PenetrationBonus (+5 at Lv10) stacked on top of Vorpal's pen=AV matching, rolling ~6 penetrations vs ANY armor — resonant did ~60 to snapjaws vs normal stones' ~30, inverting the designed trade (resonant = flat ~23 vs everything, eb_sim.py). **Fixed in MakeStone (ruling refined twice, final):** attuned stones keep **one fifth** of the level-scaled bonus (`scaledPens / 5` — +1 at Lv10, scales at wish-levels; earlier forms −5-flat and flat-1 superseded). Covers both sculpt and in-place retune (both route through MakeStone). Level text notes the trade. `[verify]` resonant vs snapjaw ≈ 1 penetration ~10 dmg; vs Cragmensch still ÷ AV-matched.
- [x] `[design]` **Resonant stones — IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-21.** "Attune Stones" toggle granted at Lv10 (`SyncAttuneAbility`, granted/revoked on level change); resonant blueprint `Cleo_TerraFirma_EphemeralStoneResonant` (cyan, `VibroWeapon ChargeUse=0` + thrown `Vorpal`); `RetuneHeldStone()` swaps the held stone in place at net-zero charge cost (guarded against the replace-handler double-sculpt); Gravel Whorl filter flips to Light-tagged projectiles + DV +2/+3 while resonant; level text and new ability card in place. Icon is a cyan-tinted reuse of the takestones tile — swap for bespoke art whenever. Spec (for reference): A second toggle ability (gated ~Lv10, tunable) that attunes your stones: they gain the adjective **{{C|resonant}}** (cyan) and vibro penetration (PV=AV), base form/name ladder unchanged. Sim grounding (`eb_sim.py`): resonant = flat ~23 avg vs any armor — Cragmensch 1 throw, Lithofex burstable, Chrome Pyramid still no (171) — while normal stones do 68 vs AV 3; a true choice, not an upgrade.
  - **Toggling retunes the stone already in hand, in place, at no charge cost** (free action) — never wastes a stone for a player who forgets to pre-toggle; auto-equip continues sculpting whichever mode is active.
  - **The whorl retunes with the stones (locked 2026-07-21):** the toggle flips the shield's filter along with the ammo. Normal whorl: deflects physical projectiles, +1/+2 AV (as shipped). Resonant whorl: deflects **Light-tagged projectiles** (laser rifle/pistol fire — verified `Attributes="Light Laser"` in game blueprints; filter = attributes contain `Light`), physical projectiles pass through, and the AV bonus becomes **+2/+4 DV (RULED 2026-07-23, supersedes the provisional +2/+3: "DV is less valuable than AV so you need more to compensate" — the full 1 AV→2 DV rate; effect + level text updated, deployed)**. Same deflect-chance formula both modes. One toggle retunes offense and defense to the same world: mass vs energy — preserves "a choice, not an upgrade." Beam attacks (Lase-type) bypass both modes (engine: no missile event) — say so in the description text.
  - Implementation notes: melee half = `VibroWeapon ChargeUse="0"` (precedent: the mod's statue fist); thrown half = `Vorpal` attribute on `ThrownWeapon` (what the engine's ÷ penetration display keys off) — **verify the thrown path in-game**. Daze rider unchanged in both modes.
  - **Division of labor:** cleanest shape is a second blueprint (`Cleo_TerraFirma_EphemeralStoneResonant` inheriting the base stone: cyan Render colors, VibroWeapon/Vorpal, flavor description) — **user offered to own the XML**; C# side picks the blueprint by toggle state and handles the in-place retune.
  - Ties into "throw-augmenting items" (design budget) as the broader late-game answer; Chrome Pyramids stay out of reach by design. **(Provenance audit 2026-07-26: "out of reach by design" is a CLAUDE READING off eb_sim.py, UNRULED — no ruling exists that Pyramids must stay unreachable.)**

### Whorl outcome scale

- [x] `[bug]` **Unequipped sculpted stones pocketed as inventory items — RULED + FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23 (active next restart):** manually unequipping an EB sculpted stone left it sitting in the pack as a possession. User ruling: "return back to the take stones option, or be destroyed" — implemented as both: at end of turn, any sculpted stone in the caster's INVENTORY is reabsorbed (charge refunded when the reserve has room, crumbles when full). Deliberately end-of-turn rather than the unequip event — throws also unequip mid-flight, and an unequip hook would refund stones actually thrown. Message "You return the stone to the earth." DRAFT. `[verify]` unequip a sculpted stone → it vanishes next turn + charge count rises; thrown stones still spend normally.
- [x] `[polish]` **Whorl message conjugation typo — FIXED + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23 (active next restart):** "your gravel whorl **send** the laser beam careening". Root cause (decomp-confirmed, `Messaging.XDidY`): the calls passed BOTH the possessive string subject AND `Actor: Object` — the engine conjugates via `Actor.GetVerb`, so a player-owned whorl got second-person verbs ("send") under a third-person subject; NPC whorls read correctly by coincidence. Fix, all nine call sites / five verbs in `Effect_GravelWhorl.cs`: verbs pre-conjugated third-person ("devours"/"tears"/"deflects"/"swats"/"sends"), `Actor: Object` → `Source: Object` (Source keeps the visibility gate `UseVisibilityOf ?? Source ?? Actor` and message routing; a null-Actor with no Source would have silently dropped the messages). `[verify]` each outcome message reads correctly in-game.
- [x] `[bug?]` **Whorl-deflected THROWN items: placement drift + fragility — RULED (b) + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23.** (The find: `PerformThrow` finishes the throw AFTER Devour/Swat/Ricochet acts — re-places the weapon at its own landing cell, overriding the outcome's placement, and the deflected rock then rolled throw-fragility and broke, undercutting "loot preserved.") Ruling: mark the projectile. New transient part `Cleo_TerraFirma_WhorlDeflectedPart` — Swat/Ricochet attach it to real thrown weapons with the outcome's landing cell; at `AfterThrownEvent` it restores that placement (if the engine moved it) and removes itself; `FallenRockPart` calls its `Restore()` FIRST (so miss-payloads/deferred pulses fire from the right cell) and skips the fragility roll while marked (a deflection is the whorl's use, not the thrower's). Added at runtime = always last in the part list, so self-removal mid-dispatch is safe. `[verify]` deflected thrown rock rests where the message says + survives (no fragility break); non-rock thrown items (javelin) also rest at the outcome's cell.
<details><summary>Superseded 2026-07-21 spec (reference)</summary>
Eaten ~50% (real items drop at feet; stones absorb +1), Deflected ~30%, Ricochet ~20% or 10%+1%/Lv (2–4 cells, −2 PV mini-attack via RollDamagePenetrations). Physical only — the engine has no refract system for non-light projectiles (verified 2026-07-21).
</details>

- [x] `[design]` **Distraction fossil → tank statue — RULED 2026-07-21: revive as taunt. BUILT** (uncommented, `Statue.Brain.Passive` removed). Residual watch item (polish pass): user note "hopefully not too strong" — watch in testing; immunity mechanic should self-balance.

Verified 2026-07-21: the commented block still compiles against the current `Distraction` part; revival = uncomment + delete `Statue.Brain.Passive = true;`. Mechanic niceties (decompiled): a successful save grants `DistractionImmunity` for 3600–8400 turns (taunt checks each enemy once — no per-turn spam, strong-willed enemies opt out cleanly), and it never affects the player (future wild stoneshapers can't taunt-lock you). Save is Willpower vs 10+Lv — another Willpower-theme touchpoint, on the enemy's side. Options: (a) delete fossil; **(c) revive as taunt — recommended**; statue stats (25 HP/Lv, wall AV, modest damage) already profile as a tank.

- [x] `[design]` **Natural weapons — RULED 2026-07-21: cosmetic middle ground. BUILT** (and repaired live 2026-07-23 — see "Raised in live-fire" below). Statue natural weapons copy the muse's weapon **tile, name, and slot only**; damage/to-hit stay on the mutation's level formula (`GetNaturalDamage`/`GetToHitBonus`). Implement in `ApplyStatueBuffs`: when the replaced part had a DefaultBehavior, copy its Render tile + DisplayName onto the haymaker instance; fall back to gravelly haymaker cosmetics when it had none. (Survey verdict that killed wholesale-keep: disease/poison/tech-organ leaks — see below.)
- [x] `[bug]` `[design]` **Inert container statue — RULED 2026-07-21, unified spec. BUILT** (all three paths; in-game verification tracked in the checklist at the bottom). One shared helper: *render a creature down into an inert, lootable statue* (fresh non-animated statue object with the creature's render/name/description; all possessions — inventory + equipped — transferred into it; lootable via Inventory + the base game's container part — verify exact part name against e.g. the Chest blueprint at implementation; contingency if containers prove hard: drop a gear pile beside the statue). Serves three paths:
  1. ✔ *Gorgon petrify* (kill path) — **implemented 2026-07-21:** shared helper `_StatueHelpers.cs` (`EntombPossessions`/`ReturnPossessions`, Chest-verified Container+Inventory recipe, all APIs precedent-checked); `Petrify()` entombs before `Die()` and unwinds if the death is prevented.
  2. *Stoneshaper natural expiry (Lv 1–9):* statue no longer reverts to a wall — it freezes into an inert container statue where it stands, lent gear entombed but recoverable. Wall-restore code and `CurWallBp` bookkeeping retire. **Accepted cost: consumed walls never come back.** Fixes the equipped-gear-obliterated bug by design.
  3. *Permanent-statue swap (Lv 10+) — NEW, ruled:* casting with a living permanent statue no longer blocked; the old statue de-animates via the same helper (gear entombed, lootable) and the new one is carved. Normal `BASE_COOLDOWN` applies per cast. Temporary statues (Lv 1–9) remain un-swappable — the lifetime-as-cooldown display already gates recasting naturally.
  4. *Swap confirmation (ruled 2026-07-21):* player-only `Popup.ShowYesNo` before a swap that de-animates a living permanent statue, naming the statue in the prompt (Sunder Mind's self-target confirm is the house precedent). Extra stakes: permanent statues keep their `Leveler` part — they gain levels over time, which a swap destroys unrecoverably; say so in the prompt wording if it fits.

  (or tfstrange until one rolls) → `tfspore` → note the number → Save and Quit → reload the

  EMPTY or different = SetStringGameState is not surviving the boot, design real storage.**

  3201 identical across mint → save-quit → reload → pure read ×3. SUSPECT (b) CONFIRMED,
  CODE INNOCENT — SetStringGameState persists fine through a real save; the cross-boot
  resets were harness-iteration sessions ending unsaved (quit-without-save loses the write
  legitimately). BUG CLOSED, no storage redesign. Rider: Tier 2's lottery motivation is
  gone — only the scav-vs-vanilla baseline still argues for it.**

### New item mechanics (seismophone)

- [ ] `[bug]` **Sound on leaving wall contact — USER FLAG 2026-08-24, same sitting ("it plays a sound when you leave the walls radius, which could get a bit spammy/annoying?"). ROOT CAUSE FOUND (decomp):** `HeightenedHearing.FireEvent("EndTurn")` stamps a hidden `HeightenedHearingEffect` on every Combat object in radius via `FastSquareSearch` — **including the listener themself at distance 0** (base filters the player out of the effect's `FinalRender`, but not out of the stamping loop). When the grant drops, the self-effect's `CheckListen()` finds the part gone → `BadListener()` → `RemoveEffect` → `GameObject.RemoveEffectAt` unconditionally plays `Effect.RemoveSound`, which returns `sfx_statusEffect_stop` for any effect dying on the player (`Effect.cs`, the type-2048 path; creature-side removals are silent because `RemoveSound` null-checks `IsPlayer`). Base never hears this because innate hearing is permanent; our conditional grant fires it on every wall-departure, plus unequip, power-off, cell death, EMP. **The beep is a base wart surfaced by the conditional: an audible death-knell for an effect the player was never shown** (its `GetDescription` returns null). **DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED IN GAME 2026-08-24, both halves:** instrumentation logged `self-effect STAMPED` at wall contact and `self-effect REMOVED` at every step-off across repeated cycles, and the user confirmed the beep lands on the step-offs ("yep"). **FIX BUILT + DEPLOYED same sitting, NO HARMONY (user: "can we do it without harmony?" — the drafted `Effect.RemoveSound`-getter prefix was reverted before ever landing):** veto the self-stamp so the noisy effect never exists on the player. Native seam found by decomp: `GameObject.ApplyEffect` runs `ApplyEffectEvent.Check` before anything lands, but the event is cascade 0 and never reaches equipment — the base workaround is **cross-object registration** (`CyberneticsAirCurrentMicrosensor`: `E.Implantee.RegisterEvent(this, <event>.ID, 0, Serialize: true)`, the Serialize flag carrying it across save/load). `Part_Seismophone.cs` now registers on the wearer at `EquippedEvent`, unregisters at `UnequippedEvent`, and its `HandleEvent(ApplyEffectEvent)` returns false for a `HeightenedHearingEffect` whose `Listener` IS the wearer — self-stamp only; another creature's hearing stamping our wearer has `Listener != wearer` and passes untouched. The mutation re-attempts the stamp each turn and is re-vetoed, cost is one type check. Compiles green, hash-verified. **Known one-shot wart:** a save made before this fix can carry one already-stamped self-effect; it dies with one last beep when the grant next drops, then silence forever. `[verify]` next boot: wall on/off cycles still grant/remove hearing (`[SEISMO]` lines), `self-stamp vetoed` appears, `self-effect STAMPED` never does, and no beep on step-off/unequip/power-off. **VERIFIED IN GAME 2026-08-24 (user: "it works, nice fix"), both halves: a dozen-plus grant/remove cycles logged, including rapid single-turn wall touches, with zero `self-effect STAMPED` lines; silence confirmed by ear. The beep bug is CLOSED; the seismophone stands fully verified.**

### New item mechanics (glowstone pair)

**Glowsphere fragmenting — USER FLOAT, unruled ("glowsphere could break into multiple fragments, potentially?").** CLAUDE recon on whether it fixes the trade:

- **The number that settles it: `XRL.World.Calendar.TurnsPerDay = 1200`** (also `TurnsPerHour = 50`, `StartOfDay = 3250`, `StartOfNight = 10000`, `TurnsPerYear = 438000`). So `MoteProperties.Fuel = 5000` is **about 4.2 in-game days of continuous light.** Generous, but finite.
- **Fragmenting does not fix the trade by quantity, and cannot.** Glowsphere light is permanent; N shards times 4.2 days is finite for every N. On the duration axis breaking a sphere is a downgrade at any fragment count. **What fragmenting genuinely buys is PLURALITY** — several simultaneous lights where there was one: a shard in the lantern plus spares, one handed to a companion, shards dropped or thrown as markers. Base `Luminous Mote` already carries `Stacker`, so a stackable shard is native. If the design leans on plurality, the fragment count is a real dial; if it leans on duration, no fragment count rescues it.
- **CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED — the lantern's payoff should be fuel, not radius.** Given the radius-8 ceiling recorded above, the ceiling-safe version is: a bare shard is the *worse* portable light (it steps down 6/5/4/3 and dies), and the lantern holds it at a **fixed** radius with no step-down plus a slowed burn. That makes the lantern wanted without touching the light economy, and it matches the item pair the user described (cage encloses shard) without needing radius 9+.
- **CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED — the locked description already promises a mechanic the glowsphere cannot do.** The shard text reads "its glow wanes in the harvest dawn": daylight-sensitive light. Burning fuel only at night, or dimming outdoors under the sun, is a behavior no glowsphere has, is legible without any tooltip, and is exactly what the user already wrote. `StartOfDay = 3250` / `StartOfNight = 10000` are the constants. This is the cleanest candidate for "why would anyone break a perfectly good glowsphere."
- **Spawn-side note (CLAUDE, flagged for the poptable pass):** base `Glowsphere` appears in `PopulationTables.xml` at Weight 20 in a general item table (line 3088) and as `Number="1-2"` in a stash entry (line 4826); `Floating Glowsphere` is spread across five more tiered floating-slot tables. Spheres are not rare, so shards inherit that availability multiplied by the fragment count. Worth a look before the count is set.


**Glowstone rulings, same sitting (user, after the fragment recon):**

- **Light-economy concern WAVED OFF — RULED (user: "light economy isn't really a thing in qud so i'm not worried about this breaking it").** The radius-8 ceiling stands as fact but is not a design constraint here; the lantern may exceed it. The ceiling-flag above is answered, not deleted (provenance).
- **Day/night fuel behavior VETOED — RULED (user: "burning fuel only at night/dimming under open sun is overcomplicating things").** The harvest-dawn proposal above is dead; the description line stays flavor.
- **Lantern > bare shard — RULED (user: "lanterns should be better than bare glowsphere shards though!").** Direction only; which axis (radius, burn rate, no step-down, any mix) is open, Claude's to sketch, numbers unruled.
- **Shards loadable into lanterns — RULED (user: "glowsphere shards should be loadable into lanterns").** Confirms the pair's core verb. Loading idiom (EnergyCellSocket-style slot vs LiquidFueledEnergyCell-style feed vs simple inventory-consume on light) is delegated plumbing, unruled.
- **Fragment count RULED (user, same sitting): 2-3 per broken sphere.**
- **Loading idiom = SOCKET — user lean, effectively settled (user: "i think socket makes the most sense," after the socket-vs-consume explain).** The shard stays a real object housed in the lantern, EnergyCellSocket pattern: load/unload/part-swap, a half-burned shard comes back out half-burned, its own Fuel and status tag keep running while housed. The lantern's better-than-bare bonus phrases as "shards burn slower while socketed" plus whatever radius hold Claude's sketch settles on.

### Box of chalk (junction kit)

- **JUNCTION KIT LANDED 2026-08-25 (closes the round-3 "need to add more pieces" gap; compiles green 0 errors, deployed 13 pngs + part).** User drew the five junction sprites on a sliced sheet; naming follows the kit's position convention (CLAUDE proposal, adopted by the user in the drawing): tee named for the plot EDGE it sits on with the stem reaching inward — `chalklineTN` = ┬ on the north edge (arms E+W+S), TS = ┴, TE = ┤ (east edge, stem west), TW = ├ — and `chalklineX` = the four-way. **Five ORIGINALS also changed in the same export (N/NE/NW/SW/W, strokes repositioned, same pixel counts) — DELIBERATE, user ruling 2026-08-25: "yes, i had to touch these up so they matched in game."** `PieceFor` gains the junction branches ahead of the straight checks (4 arms = X, 3 arms = the tee whose stem faces the missing side); the 3-4-arm straight fallback is gone. **Asset workflow established: source of truth is `design/tiles/chalklines.aseprite`** (13 named slices, 16x24 grid; `aseprite -b <file> --split-slices --save-as <Textures>\{slice}.png` re-exports the whole kit; `design/` never ships). `stoneandbees.aseprite` moved out of shipping Textures the same sitting (dev copy moved by the user, chalk-vfx copy git-mv'd to `design/tiles/`). One slice in the .aseprite is still named `chalkline TW` with a stray space (output renamed by hand this once; user fixes the slice name at leisure). `[verify]` next boot: draw a plus and a T — junction cells wear the tee/cross sprites, arms meet neighbors with no seams, straights/corners unchanged, no MODERROR. **VERIFIED IN GAME 2026-08-25 (user: "verified working!", screenshot: "CAVES OF QUD" chalked across a grass zone — tees, corners, and straights all flush, letterforms clean).**
  - **JUNCTION SEAM, user screenshot same day — DIAGNOSED (pixel-proven) + FIXED BY USER ART (deployed hash-verified 13/13).** Root cause: horizontal ROLE rows vs fixed junction bars — the run-walker reads a ┴ tee's north stem as "turn north," so both arms ALWAYS role bottom-edge (row 11), while every tee/X bar was drawn at the top-edge row 13; deterministic 2px step at every ┴, and ├/┤/┼ arms could seam the same way when their run's far end turned north. Three dispositions offered (redraw TS at 11 / picker inherits junction row / unify all rows); **user took unification, their pixels: ALL horizontal bars now row 11, ALL verticals col 8, entire 13-piece kit — seams impossible by construction, roles only pick dash patterns now.** Round-2's "N row 12 / S row 11, cols 7/8" pixel map is HISTORY as of this art pass. Workflow note: the user's aseprite save defaulted back to Downloads; stale-source check (compare Downloads vs `design/tiles` mtimes) is part of the re-export ritual now. `[verify]` next boot: redraw the screenshot shapes — T and branch junctions meet their arms flush, zero steps anywhere. **VERIFIED same day by the CAVES OF QUD screenshot above.** Final art rounds (e28f255 junctions, e857899 bases) carried the user's kit design rule: **junction dash patterns are SUPERSETS of their base lines** (user 2026-08-25: "making sure the junction lines match up with the base lines, so that adjustments don't directly affect the line's texture") — a retile that promotes a straight to a junction keeps every shared dash in place and only adds the new arm, so the ripple never reshuffles a run's texture.

### Earthen Barrage

- [x] `[bug]` **SUBSCRIBER REPORT 2026-08-12 (relayed near-verbatim by the user): "Take Stones (x/y)" ambiguity + "needing to toggle" after pickup — DIAGNOSED + BOTH FIXES BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles green 0 errors, Objects.xml strict-valid, hash-verified — active next restart).** The subscriber reverse-engineered the state machine themselves and was right on both counts: the counter reads the RESERVE only (equipped stone lives outside it), so "0/3" meant both armed-with-empty-reserve and truly dry; and the pickup path missed every ruled re-arm trigger. Pickup mechanism: a stone taken off the ground lands in inventory, the end-of-turn reabsorb (07-23 "held intention" ruling) refunds the charge but never re-arms, AND the refund poisons the regen backstop (its ran-dry QoL literally checks `Charges == 1` right after its own increment — a refund-to-1 makes the next tick land on 2 and the check never fires; stuck unarmed until a manual toggle cycle). **Fixes, both RULED at the 2026-08-12 sitting: (1) display = "(x/y+1)" — USER'S OWN FORMAT ("0/3+1 makes sense as like 'i have no stones in reserve and 1 equipped'"), "+1" suffix while a sculpted stone rides the thrown slot, synced on throw/EndTurn so it can't go stale. (2) pickup re-arm via TakenEvent, the thorn-dodging shape (user raised the can't-unequip worry; converged on event-split): new `Scripts/Part_StoneRearmOnTake.cs` (`Cleo_TerraFirma_StoneRearmOnTakePart`) on the EphemeralStone blueprint (resonant inherits) pokes `OnStoneTaken` — decomp-verified that TakenEvent fires ONLY on the take-from-world flow (sculpts arrive via ReceiveObject→AddedToInventoryEvent), so picking up one of our own stones with toggle on + empty slot equips it straight back (lease refreshed), while a DELIBERATE unequip-into-pack fires no TakenEvent and keeps the 07-23 reabsorb ruling untouched.** Scope guards, recorded: plain rocks (IsAnyStone) deliberately excluded — auto-wielding ordinary loot would hijack scavenging; a dry thrower still self-heals on the regen tick. The poisoned `Charges == 1` backstop LEFT AS-RULED: with pickup handled at the event, no realistic path reaches reabsorb-while-dry-and-unarmed (sculpted stones crumble in 50-100r; nobody trades them). Dials flagged, unruled: pickup-equip is SILENT (the take message already printed; a "you ready the stone" line is available if wanted) · an attuned thrower picking up a NORMAL stone equips it as-is, no auto-retune (free retune via the Attune toggle exists). `[verify]` next boot: throw dry → pick up own stone → it equips + label reads "(0/3+1)" · deliberate unequip into pack still reabsorbs WITHOUT re-equipping · dry throw drops the "+1" · label at full reserve while armed reads "(3/3+1)" (or level equivalent). **SAME-DAY LIVE-CATCH + CORRECTION (user, first live session on the build: "equipping anything in your thrown slot while a sculpted pebble is in your hand fully deletes the stone without adding it to your reserve pouch" — CONFIRMED MINE, FIXED + REDEPLOYED same sitting, compiles green, hash-verified):** the "TakenEvent fires only on take-from-world" premise was WRONG — decomp: the unequip-to-inventory flow ends in `eCommandFreeTakeObject`, which IS a `CommandTakeObject` (EnergyCost 0), so displaced equipment also arrives via PerformTake/TakenEvent. The ungated part re-equipped the stone into the slot the engine was mid-clearing on every swap; the stone died in the fight (log signature: ZERO reabsorb lines all session — it never rested in inventory). Fix: the part snapshots `BeginBeingTaken` (fires on the item BEFORE it leaves its old context) — in-a-cell = true ground pickup, acts; equipped/container = shuffle, stays out, the 07-23 reabsorb governs. Positive side-effect of the gate: container takes don't auto-equip either (rummaging ≠ stooping). Ground-pickup re-arm was CONFIRMED WORKING live before the correction (multiple `[EB] pickup re-arm` log lines, user's own session) and the gate does not touch that path. `[verify]` re-run: swap another thrown weapon over a held stone → stone lands in pack and reabsorbs at end of turn (refund below cap / crumble at cap, message either way) · manual unequip-to-pack same · ground pickup still re-arms.

### Calcify

- [x] `[design]` **NPC-caster gap — RULED batch-now + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23** (user un-parked it: "let's knock calcify off the list"). Two halves: (1) AI advertisement on `AIGetDefensiveAbilityListEvent` + `AIGetRetreatAbilityListEvent` ONLY — deliberately never offensive, because calcified stone is opaque both ways (Force Wall advertises offensively only because its owner can shoot out through its own fields); threat = the event's target, else Force Wall's nearest-visible-hostile retreat scan, passed through `E.Add` so the command carries it. (2) NPC cell-picking `PickNpcWallLine`: perpendicular line across the approach direction, anchored at the caster's adjacent cell (honoring the walls-start-adjacent rule), extended along both orthogonals to the wall count; occupied cells skipped by the placer — partial lines accepted like partial player casts. `[verify]` wish Calcify onto a creature and watch it wall off its attacker when pressed (the appendix "NPC Fissure/Gorgon casters" item gains a calcifier sibling).

Two things fall out of it: the mason gets its contrast back, and the LEGENDARY finally reads as pink over dark grey, which is what the user asked for at the very first pack (`MakeLegendary` paints ColorString and leaves DetailColor alone). Supersedes every earlier colour pass this day — the interim brown/white base, the carrier flip, and the channel argument under them.** *(Original finding, for the record:)* **CLAUDE BUG, found 2026-08-15.** After the carrier flip the mason is `ColorString="&y"` over an inherited `DetailColor="y"`: light grey on light grey, no contrast, so it reads as a smudge where every other role is two-tone. Independent of which way the grey pair eventually goes. **Options the user was weighing when they paused:** (a) keep as deployed (chassis `&K` over `y`) and give the mason its own detail override · (b) `DetailColor="K"` for every carver with mains keeping their role color, which fixes the mason as a side effect · (c) chassis fully dark (`&K` over `K`) as a neutral ground so only the three roles carry color. *Also on record: the user reported the stonecarver main as light grey while the deployed build already had it dark — the flip had landed but the game only loads mods at launch, so they were judging a stale build. Legendaries untouched by the user's explicit carve-out.
