### Quarriers roster (etch tools)

  - [x] `[design]` **PROPER ETCH TOOLS BUILT 2026-08-15** (user: "it occured to me currently all stonecarvers are using blunted chisels and splintered mallets... maybe copper or steel?"). **RULED: steel, with carbide as the ceiling; the PLAYER KEEPS THE BLUNTED PAIR** — which is where its meaning comes from, since the starter tools are characterised as worn out and that only reads once the guild carries better. **COPPER CONSIDERED AND DROPPED (Claude counter, user did not push back):** copper chisels are historically real (Egypt cut limestone with them) but copper is SOFTER than what the blunted pair is already made of, and in Qud's ladder copper is figurine-and-wire material, not tool material — it would have been a downgrade wearing an upgrade's name. **ZERO NEW MECHANICS: the user's "lower chances for etching to have messed up tiles" was already built** by their own 2026-08-07 graffiti-law ruling. `Part_Etchable` slip chance is `(GARBLE_TIER_CEILING - pairTier) * GARBLE_PCT_PER_TIER` = `(6 - tier) * 4`%, with **pair tier = `min(striker, edge)`** ("a carve is only as clean as its worse half"). So the gradient falls straight out of Tier tags: **blunted pair 24% of characters garbled · steel 16% · carbide 8%.** **Built:** `Steel Chisel`/`Steel Mallet` (inherit `Steel Dagger`/`Steel Hammer`, tier 2) and `Carbide Chisel`/`Carbide Mallet` (inherit `Dagger4`/`Cudgel4`, tier 4, matching base carbide gear). **They INHERIT base weapons rather than tagging them** — tagging `Steel Dagger` directly would make every steel dagger in Qud an etch tool, the exact widening the rubble-cover ruling refused. **Rolled via `Cleo_TerraFirma_EtchTools`, a WEIGHTED table not a `{ownertier}` ladder, and it is the one thing in the kit that does not scale** — a per-tier ladder needs a table for every tier a carver can spawn at and a missing rung fails silently; with two NPC rungs, weights give the same effect and cannot break. That line becomes `EtchTools {ownertier}` if the ladder ever grows. Edge and striker roll SEPARATELY on purpose: mismatched pairs carve at the lesser tier, which the slip law already handles. **Carbide weight 10 is a CLAUDE DRAFT**, kept low because a carbide chisel is a 1d8 weapon and a pack of them is a different fight. Four descriptions DRAFT, signoff owed. **IMPROVISED PENALTY ADDED same sitting, on the user's clarification ("i meant better chances comparatively to a carbide dagger and hammer being used in place of it") — and it exposed that the tool ladder had been COSMETIC against improvisation.** `GetEtchToolTier` counted any ShortBlades edge and any Cudgel striker at FACE TIER, so a carbide dagger etched exactly as cleanly as a carbide chisel and the `Cleo_TerraFirma_EtchTool` tag bought nothing at all. Fixed with `IMPROVISED_TIER_PENALTY = 2`, written as a PENALTY on borrowed weapons rather than a BONUS on proper tools **so that nothing already tuned moves**: blunted stays at its ruled 24%, steel 16%, carbide 8%, and only improvisation gets worse. Clamped at 0 so a tier-0 club cannot push garble past the 24% ceiling. New comparative: **carbide dagger + hammer now carve at 16%** (like steel tools) **and steel dagger + hammer at 24%** (like the blunted pair). A borrowed weapon always carves like a real tool two rungs below it. CLAUDE DIAL, awaiting ratification alongside the two garble constants it sits with.

### Quarriers roster (kits)

  - [x] `[design]` **KITS BUILT 2026-08-15.** Shape RULED by the user against Claude's fixed-list pitch: *"rather than use player base armor, we could do something like generate tier appropriate armor with a few items biased to roll each time."* That is the base game's own idiom and `HumanoidEquipment` is the model. **The mechanism that makes it work: `{ownertier}`**, a substitution token resolving tier off the OWNING creature, so one table scales itself with no per-tier duplicates. `GetTier()` = explicit `Tier` tag, else `(Level - 1) / 5 + 1`. **Our chassis is Level 10 with no Tier tag, so carvers currently roll TIER 2 gear** — that coupling is now load-bearing, since raising the roster level raises what they wear, and the level is still a Claude placeholder for the numbers sitting. Wired via `<tag Name="InventoryPopulationTable">`, the same tier-child idiom already noted for the snapjaw miner. **Spine (`Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarrier Kit`), certain every time:** a rolled etch-tool pair + waterskin + 2-5 torches, then ROLLED tier armor (75%, the `HumanoidEquipment` dynamic/static 40:60 split) and tier shoes (60%). Entries without a `Chance` in a `pickeach` group fire every time, which is the "biased to roll each time" half. **The chisel+mallet pair means the etch gate passes BY CONSTRUCTION**, so the parked idle-etch brain part becomes possible the moment kits exist. **Role tells, one item each** (the mutation is the job, the item is only the tell): **hewer = Vinewood Sap Mask** — chosen over a pickaxe DELIBERATELY, since `DiggingTool` installs Digging and answers `PathAsBurrowerEvent` and an armed hewer would tunnel out of a hand-painted warren; the mask is AV 0, pure characterisation, and says the same thing. **mason = `Cleo_Stonecarver_Maquette`** (RULED by the user, superseding Claude's bubble level: "give the mason a figurine") — the mod's OWN table, the exact one in the player calling kit, so a mason carries what a player stonecarver carries. **outrider = cobblefur bracer**, which drags an ecology in behind it (they hunt cobble baboons for the pelt in the same canyons, and `AddsRep Baboons -100` makes the baboons hate them for it). **Overseer takes the bare spine and nothing bespoke**, per the ruled goatfolk structure: the hero inherits the ordinary kit and the EPITHET tops it up. That is what keeps the byname load-bearing instead of decorative, and it finally gives the `lode` material perk something to bite (richer gear populations, by name alone). Closes the "baseline kit under the grant OPEN" item. **Figurines scoped to the mason only**, not smeared across the pack as a loot floor; a `Figurines {ownertier}` line at low chance is all it would take if that ever changes. **Open, surfaced BY the kit decision:** AV on a Level 10 chassis is soft, and now that gear quality is coupled to level, "soft and numerous vs tougher and fewer" is a real fork rather than a dial — numbers sitting.

Now a plain tag, inherited by the whole tree. ### Quarriers faction (name + structure)

- [x] `[design]` **FACTION NAME RULED + BUILT 2026-08-15: `Quarriers' Guild`** (user, choosing it over Stonecarvers'). The reasoning was the user's own cobble-baboon principle turned on this decision: "stonecarver" already names the calling, the creature and the workstream, and a fourth job for one word is the collision that killed "gravel baboon" next to "Gravel Whorl". The institution gets its own word; "stonecarver" keeps meaning the person. **Claude's 07-26 lean was the OPPOSITE (Stonecarvers'-over-Quarriers') and is superseded** — it was recorded before the roster existed, when the word had fewer jobs. **Period RULED same exchange (user: "post-sultanate") and the engine states it natively:** `Old="false"` is the flag separating contemporary institutions from relics, and both base guilds carry it, so "guild" was already doing the period signalling. **BUILT:** new `TerraFirma/XML/Factions.xml`, structure copied verbatim from the base game's only two guilds (plural possessive, no article in DisplayName, `FormatWithArticle` supplying "the", HistoricalSignificance 1); faction key `Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarriers`; emblem is **`stonecarver.png`, the HUMAN body, RULED 2026-08-15**, colors `y` over `K`. *(The mole emblem lasted about an hour: the user picked `stonerat.png`, then found it faced the wrong way and asked for it "flipped." Claude read that as COLOR-inverted and swapped the channels — answering a question nobody asked — when the user meant literally MIRRORED. User settled it the simpler way: "just use the regular stonecarver.png rather than stonerat." So the human fronts the guild after all, and the color swap is reverted. Still available if the mole is ever wanted back: a mirrored COPY of the sprite as its own emblem file, since the tile is referenced only here and a copy would not disturb the mole creatures sharing the original.)* Three guilds is now the whole set in Qud. Carver chassis Brain now reads `Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarriers-100`, replacing the inherited `Humanoids-100`, and every role inherits it. **Description + display text SCHEDULED TO THEIR OWN PASS, 2026-08-15 (user: "description and display text can be resolved in it's own pass") — still the user's, still deliberately undrafted; this is no longer a blocker on the faction, it is a text-pass item.** Home: `design/writing-sittings-1.0.md`. Placeholders + open calls filed at the entry: rep jingles currently REUSE the Farmers' Guild pair. **Reframed 2026-08-15: this is barely debt at all** — sharing a jingle is base-game practice, not a compromise (`sultanCult` is used by 5 factions, `village` by 4, out of ~62 distinct pairs), so the only real question is which existing pair characterises the guild, and bespoke audio is optional rather than owed. **User asked to judge by ear; the clips are packed inside Unity's `resources.assets` with no loose ogg on disk, so extraction would need AssetRipper — but PLAYING them needs nothing.** Built `tfjingle` (cycles the candidates, one per wish, naming each before it plays) + `tfjingleneg` (the sour half of the current pick, since a faction picks a PAIR). Candidates loaded: `mopango` (Claude lean — underground dwellers, and the tracker's own mopango-symmetry thread pairs with the mole emblem ruling) · `cragmensch` (stone people, closest material match) · `barathrumites` · `farmersGuild` (current) · `merchantsGuild` · `hermits` · `issachariTribe`. **RULED BY EAR 2026-08-15 (user, after auditioning: "definitely cragsmensch") — the guild takes the `cragmensch` pair, replacing the interim Farmers' borrow. The item is CLOSED, not deferred: sharing is base practice, so there is no bespoke-audio debt left hanging. Side effect on record: quarriers and base cragmensch are now indistinguishable by ear on a rep change. Reads as kinship between stone things rather than as a collision, but it is a real consequence of the pick.** · **`waterritual` RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "join on water ritual should also follow the same pattern") — and the ABSENCE of a node is the ruling. The two base guilds DISAGREE, which is the finding:** Merchants' writes `Join="false"` alongside `BuyMostValuableItem` because it has special ritual behaviour to declare; **Farmers' writes no `waterritual` node at all** and takes the defaults. Quarriers have no special ritual behaviour either, so they take the Farmers' shape and `WaterRitualJoin` stays TRUE. **Quarriers are recruitable, now deliberately rather than by omission.** *(Meaning of the flag CORRECTED 2026-08-15 by the user, against a Claude misreading: `Join` gates whether THEY join YOU, not whether the player can join the guild. Decomp `WaterRitualJoinParty.Awake`: the option shows when the faction allows it, the speaker is not already player-led, and the speaker carries no `WaterRitual`/`NoJoin` xTag; base Merchants' Guild sets false, i.e. merchants do not follow you home. Cost ≈ 200 rep scaled by the level gap, floor 50.)* The real open call is whether quarriers are recruitable at all, and whether the OVERSEER differs from the rank and file — the per-creature `NoJoin` xTag makes that split cheap. Sits next to the open "water ritual on the overseer" thread · **`interests` RULED + BUILT 2026-08-15 (user: "buying ruins is good, selling desertcanyons and underground works")** — the flag is dissolved. Recon that framed the call: `interests` is the water-ritual SECRETS trade, and `WillBuy`/`WillSell` are independent, so the buy/sell split is the characterising half (base Farmers' Guild is one buy-only line, `humanoid,settlement`, and it tells you everything about farmers). Tag→prose is a hardcoded switch in `FactionInterest.GenerateDescription` (~70 tags); the grammar is that a creature/faction tag pairs with `lair` or `settlement` to make "the locations of X lairs", which is why those two dominate the base file. A `Description` attribute can override the prose, so invented tags are possible (base does it 3x); `MatchAny` and `Inverse` also exist. **Built: SELL `desertcanyons` + `underground`, BUY `ruins`.** The read: quarriers sell their own domain, the canyons and everything under them, and pay for leads on worked stone they have not reached. **Ruins direction deliberately survives the 2026-08-12 calling change** that swapped ruins lore for canyons — the calling stopped being FROM the ruins; the guild still wants to hear about them. *Unproven caveat kept on record: the tag→prose switch and the buy/sell blurb are decomp-confirmed, but the code that actually OFFERS the secrets is not in the current decomp dump, so "these tags gate which secrets get traded" is the evident reading and not something Claude has watched happen* · feelings ship NEUTRAL to everyone rather than inventing a politics, with only the Girsh/Entropic hostility both base guilds state · **whether a pack should open friendly, wary or hostile to the player is NOT decided** (currently neutral at 0 starting rep).

### Creature roster (item wiring)

- [x] `[bug]` **WHORL-vs-GLOVES DOUBLE-RESOLVE — USER REPORT 2026-08-23 ("sculpted pebbles devoured by your gravel whorl seem to be stuck - can't be thrown, can't be removed? could be from the catching gloves also, check both"), DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same sitting (compiles green, hash-verified x3; active next restart).** Both checked per the ask, and it IS both: whorl (effect, WantEvent pass) and gloves (`RegisterEvent` on the wearer, registered pass) ride the SAME `DefenderMissileHitEvent` in SEPARATE dispatch passes, and NEITHER checked `E.Done` or object validity — so one resolver acted on a projectile the other had already resolved: the gloves equip a caught stone into the thrown slot, the whorl devour then obliterates it IN the slot (or the gloves catch an already-obliterated corpse), leaving a dead reference the slot can neither throw nor release. **Same defect class as the grinder's equipped-pebble feed bug (2026-08-22).** Fix, three layers: (1) whorl entry bails on `E.Done` / invalid projectile · (2) gloves gate gains `!E.Done` + `Validate` + not-graveyarded · (3) **SLOT HEAL in EB's EndTurn (this also recovers the user's stuck character on their first turn after restart):** an invalid thrown-slot object is cleared from its body part (`[EB] cleared a dead thrown-slot reference` line) so the same turn's re-arm proceeds — the grinder bug's "bail forever at `GetFirstThrownWeapon() != null`" class is now belt-covered for ANY future slot-death path. **Precedence note, dial not ruling: with both guards in, FIRST DISPATCHED WINS by engine order; if the user ever wants a ruled precedence (whorl outranks gloves or the reverse), it is a one-line gate.** `[verify]` next boot: stuck character heals on turn one (log line + slot usable) · baboon-thrown sculpted stone vs whorl+gloves resolves ONCE (devoured OR caught, never both, `[GVW]`/`[GLOV]` lines agree) · catch-into-slot still works with the whorl down.
